Canonical repository: `Parveen117/Recognition-Kernel-Framework`; branch: `main`.
Index update: 20 November 2026; integrated MR-04–MR-07, R3–R4, SS1, SE1–SE49, SP1 and Theorems 78–82; added the PG1–PG482 Sanskrit grammar source, verification and mathematical construction stages. The canonical operator engine remains version 0.5.

## Integration and verification status

The [1 November integration record](research-history/PENDING_WORK_2026_11_01.md)
reconciles every branch in the recorded snapshot, preserves all scientific source
bytes and resolves the historical engine replay mismatch by using the archived
Node 22.16.0 runtime. Earlier stage reports remain dated records. The current
Python 3.12 navigation result recorded for that integration is passing;
Navigation reruns for PG14–PG408 are deferred as recorded below. RH,
global eta-zero positivity, K0 and the
Yang–Mills mass gap remain open; research integration does not close them.

## Active operator-engine route

[Operator Workbench](operator_foundation/README.md) is the single active development source for the shared operator engine. Its code, operative proofs and tests are self-contained; external repository names in lineage records are attribution rather than runtime dependencies.

Read the [native primitive order](operator_foundation/theory/NATIVE_PRIMITIVE_ORDER.md), [algebra and observer contracts](operator_foundation/theory/01_NATIVE_ALGEBRA.md), [completion and memory](operator_foundation/theory/02_COMPLETION_AND_MEMORY.md), [Laurent/jet repair](operator_foundation/theory/NATIVE_LAURENT_JET_REPAIR.md), [Pāṇinian symbolic compiler](operator_foundation/theory/PANINIAN_OPERATOR_COMPILER.md), [automatic normal basis and regular action](operator_foundation/theory/AUTOMATIC_REGULAR_MODEL.md), then [weighted operator completion and certified response](operator_foundation/theory/WEIGHTED_OPERATOR_COMPLETION.md).

The [canonical-home declaration](operator_foundation/CANONICAL_HOME.json), [source index](operator_foundation/SOURCE_INDEX.md), and [master certificate](operator_foundation/audit/MASTER_CERTIFICATE.json) bind this route. The public source archive at `operator_foundation/sources/rkf_reference` includes the corrected T50/T54 edition and full Morphic/Pāṇinian source directories. Use that corrected source where the older root version differs. This does not silently overwrite old source-pinned files.

## SP1: cyclic return, transported Smriti and observable event count

[SP1](research/spanda_recognition/README.md) develops a supplied full-family
affine memory extension motivated by questions about dance, activity and
recognition. Its [six proof groups](research/spanda_recognition/THEOREM.md)
identify the reset obstruction `[b]` in `M/im(I-H)` for a cycle `m -> Hm+b`.
Over a rational algebra, nonzero obstruction excludes every finite-period
memory state. A finite cut-field carrier admits an invariant scalar event
count; its accessibility to an observer has an exact row-space criterion.
MR-04 supplies future-bank closure after known affine offsets are removed.

A three-level jet example returns its source after four quarter-turn steps
while retaining `n epsilon e_2 + binomial(n,2) epsilon^2 e_1`. Its construction
commutes with zero-specialization, which nevertheless hides both obstruction
and count. [Sources](research/spanda_recognition/SOURCES.md),
[dependency pins](research/spanda_recognition/SOURCE_PINS.json),
[scope](research/spanda_recognition/SCOPE.md) and
[actual validation](research/spanda_recognition/VALIDATION.md) separate the
written proof, direct exact controls, philosophical motivation and existing
mathematical lineage. No physical time law or external novelty is asserted.
The shared engine and frozen evidence remain unchanged.

## PG1: Sanskrit grammar before implementation

[PG1](research/panini_grammar/README.md) supplies a pinned **3,983-record
Sanskrit sūtrapāṭha**, all eight chapters and 32 pādas, a **120-sūtra
foundation register** with IAST and the fourteen Māheśvara formulas,
source/commentary attribution, a grammar-state specification and worked
derivations. It separates text inventory from interpreted and domain-closed
rules. Full Sanskrit grammar and a new calculator application remain open.

The [grammar specification](research/panini_grammar/GRAMMAR_SPEC.md) and
[source reading](research/panini_grammar/SOURCES.md) distinguish it-lopa from
whole-affix lopa, equal-strength conflict from a global rule-number priority,
and rule-relative asiddha visibility from history deletion. They qualify
T80's Jaccard model of antaratama and its manually supplied records without
altering frozen theorem/result bytes. The [coverage map](research/panini_grammar/COVERAGE_AND_GATES.md)
includes the existing T58–T68 and T77–T80 domains; [validation](research/panini_grammar/VALIDATION.md)
records textual integrity separately from finite computation.

[PG2: pending grammar verification](research/panini_grammar/verification/README.md)
adds an attributed audit of 23,589 publisher scope edges, six commentary
adjudications of 346 structural flags, seven it-saṃjñā review records with
compound-marker identity, query-relative 1.1.56–59 contracts, conditional
priority witnesses and lexical diagnostics over 2,229 root entries, with the
30 gaṇasūtra directives in the same source retained separately.
Its 79 local reference cases and three direct legacy-interface probes do not
establish a general Sanskrit oracle or grammar completeness. Raw Sanskrit
scope inference, a general resolver and calculator integration remain open;
historical engine/theorem/certificate files are unchanged.

[PG3: root preprocessing after T81/T82](research/panini_grammar/root_progression/README.md)
adds source-bounded 6.1.64/65 root substitutions, 7.1.58 num licensing with
1.1.47 placement, and a separate 8.3.24/8.4.58 phonetic query. It retains
original sound and compound-marker identity. Across 2,229 root entries,
marker-only, preprocessed and query headword agreements are 1,703, 1,844 and
2,081 respectively; 146 query differences and two unresolved teaching forms
remain. These are lexical diagnostics, not grammar coverage. Its 45 local
cases and attributed [source review](research/panini_grammar/root_progression/SOURCE_REVIEW.md)
explicitly supersede PG2's jhi deletion and T82's lakāra expectations, and
qualify T81's vowel savarṇa table. Frozen predecessor packets are retained;
full grammar, independent soundness, CI, main merge and calculator integration
remain open or deferred.

[PG4: executable scopes and further root derivations](research/panini_grammar/scoped_derivations/README.md)
binds 19 reviewed records to source terms and applies seven commentary policies
to 347 inherited edges. It extends existing-root nasal processing, tuk options,
contact rules with explicit prohibitions, voicing and the original-root domain
of 8.2.78. Its new scope correction excludes inherited पदस्य at 8.2.78 under
the adopted Tattvabodhinī reading. There are 71 local cases, including 11
intentional unresolved/partial outcomes; the lexical diagnostic has 2,219
agreements, eight differences and two unresolved teaching forms. This is not
all-sūtra compilation or a Sanskrit soundness oracle. Source-bound records,
reused operations and full rule completeness are distinguished in the
[remaining-work register](research/panini_grammar/scoped_derivations/REMAINING.md).
Frozen predecessor packets, the operator engine and the calculator are unchanged.

[PG5: vikaraṇa selection and bounded laṭ derivations](research/panini_grammar/vikarana_lat/README.md)
adds 39 source-bound records and 28 identified lexical entries, including
selection examples across all ten gaṇas and one corrected anuvṛtti reading
at 7.1.3. Complete classical thematic laṭ paths cover 13 roots, 18 pada
paradigms, 162 person-number cells and 198 optional/mandatory branches.
The stage distinguishes śap/śa marker effects, trigger-relative guṇa
prohibition, luk/ślu, the two cur/ṇic aṅgas and the local T66 edhe path
correction. Forty commentary word cases, 25 selection cases, 22 expected
refusals and 44 direct controls are separate from the full comparison with
pinned Vidyut output. Neither comparison agreement nor record counts
establish all-sūtra coverage or a Sanskrit validity oracle. The
[remaining register](research/panini_grammar/vikarana_lat/REMAINING.md) starts
with non-thematic stems, infixes and reduplication; the
[validation record](research/panini_grammar/vikarana_lat/VALIDATION.md) preserves
the source, scope and CI/merge boundaries. This is a linguistic adapter,
not a new operator or calculator theorem.

[PG6: non-thematic laṭ and retained visibility](research/panini_grammar/non_thematic_lat/README.md)
completes śnu/u/śnā paths for ten more identified roots: 17 pada paradigms,
153 cells and 167 branches. Seventeen new source-bound records and three
local scope decisions support distinct guṇa triggers, cluster-sensitive
vowel junctions, optional/mandatory u-loss, weak śnā, śru's coupled
substitution, and a specific 1.1.58/8.2.79 kur length query. The ordered
phonetic view distinguishes earlier ṇatva from later restored dental n.
There are 53 literal word cases, eight expected refusals and 54 direct
controls; the 153-cell software comparison remains separate from traditional
source evidence. [Source readings](research/panini_grammar/non_thematic_lat/SOURCE_REVIEW.md)
and [validation](research/panini_grammar/non_thematic_lat/VALIDATION.md) retain
the exact bounds. [Remaining work](research/panini_grammar/non_thematic_lat/REMAINING.md)
starts with śnam infix ownership, class 7 strong/weak forms and reduplication.
General grammar soundness, operator lifting, CI and main merging remain open
or deferred.

[PG7: owned śnam infixes and class-7 laṭ](research/panini_grammar/snam_lat/README.md)
completes a declared profile for 23 identified roots: 32 pada paradigms,
288 cells and 330 branches. The root-with-mit constituent retains base-root
and affix ownership through placement, root-n deletion, strong/weak śnam
and ordered phonetic changes. Eleven source-bound records include a local
nitya relation and an attributed 8.4.65 scope decision; optional consonant
doubling remains outside the spelling profile. Eighty-five literal word
witnesses, 13 expected refusals and 63 controls are separate from the
288-cell software comparison. [Source review](research/panini_grammar/snam_lat/SOURCE_REVIEW.md)
and [validation](research/panini_grammar/snam_lat/VALIDATION.md) preserve those
boundaries. The [coverage audit](research/panini_grammar/snam_lat/COVERAGE.md)
separates 3,983 catalogued records, 80 distinct source-bound IDs in PG4–PG7,
the frozen root diagnostic, and 46 completed root entries / 603 laṭ cells
across PG5–PG7. No overall grammar-completion percentage is claimed.
[Remaining work](research/panini_grammar/snam_lat/REMAINING.md) starts with
bhuj/tṛh and adādi/juhotyādi; general grammar and operator lifting remain open.
CI and main merging remain deferred.

## PG10: further juhotyādi and vowel-final adādi present paths

[PG10](research/panini_grammar/present_expansion/README.md) adds 38 identified

Eleven juhotyādi roots retain copy/root ownership, cluster-sensitive
junctions, distinct pṝ/pṛ paths and bhī/hāk options. Twenty-seven adādi

The īṭ constituent belongs to the ending; marker memory, actual ending
substitution and apit eligibility remain explicit.

entries lack separate full-word witnesses in the selected commentaries;

## PG11: lexical present paths and owned ending substitutions

[PG11](research/panini_grammar/lexical_present/README.md) adds 40 identified
roots, 45 licensed pada paradigms, 405 present cells and 420 branches.
Thirty-nine further adādi roots include han, dviṣ/duh/dih/lih, cakṣ,
īḍ/īś/śī, brū, vid, mṛj and the lexical abhyasta group. The sole-vowel
juhotyādi root ṛ retains its source tag and explicit classical-use license.
iṭ/īṭ belong to the ending; ruṭ belongs to the actual jh substitute.
Optional vid endings and brū/āh's coupled substitutions retain the
original present features and teaching forms.

[Source review](research/panini_grammar/lexical_present/SOURCE_REVIEW.md)
binds 43 records, ten local scope overlays and six retained source
adjudications. Repeated source words are bound by occurrence and case.
[Validation](research/panini_grammar/lexical_present/VALIDATION.md) records
245 literal word cases across 39 roots, 40 refusals and 107 direct controls.
The separate 405-cell / 420-branch comparison has zero differences. New
pṛj has no separate exact full-word witness in the selected passages;
the six inherited PG10 word-witness gaps also remain open.

[PG11 coverage](research/panini_grammar/lexical_present/COVERAGE_STATUS.json)
is 131 / 2,229 root entries, 166 paradigms, 1,494 present cells and 1,643
branches, with 186 bounded definitions / 142 distinct sūtra IDs across
PG4–PG11. Class 2 covers 68 of 76 lexical rows; class 3 covers 15 of 26
entries under the declared profiles.
[Remaining work](research/panini_grammar/lexical_present/REMAINING.md)
includes prefix/context and register-sensitive entries and wider grammar.
PG1–PG10 remain frozen. Full native operator lifting, typed
zero-specialization compatibility and quantum-calculator integration are
open; hosted CI and main merging remain deferred. No overall grammar
completion percentage is assigned.

## PG12: further śnu, u and śnā present paths

[PG12](research/panini_grammar/nasal_present/README.md) adds 97 identified
roots, 124 licensed pada paradigms, 1,116 present cells and 1,316 branches:
22 further svādi, eight tanādi and 67 kryādi entries. Owned root n-lopa,
grah/jyā saṃprasāraṇa and khav's ūṭh substitution retain the intermediate
sounds, their source identities and ordered history. Four tanādi root-guṇa
options compose with affix strength and actual cluster-sensitive u-loss.
The expanded bounded ṇatva window retains tṛp/kṣubh's prohibitions and
kṣubh's exact local śnā identity permission after ā changes.

[Source review](research/panini_grammar/nasal_present/SOURCE_REVIEW.md)
binds 22 records, one scope overlay and seven retained source decisions,
including the narrow pvādi endpoint, register differences and deferred
śṝñ membership. [Validation](research/panini_grammar/nasal_present/VALIDATION.md)
records 109 literal-word cases across 73 new roots, 37 refusals and 124
direct controls. The separate 1,116-cell / 1,316-branch comparison has
zero differences. Twenty-four new entries lack separate exact full-word
witnesses; the seven earlier PG10/PG11 gaps also remain open.

[PG12 coverage](research/panini_grammar/nasal_present/COVERAGE_STATUS.json)
is 228 / 2,229 root entries, 290 paradigms, 2,610 present cells and 2,959
branches, with 208 bounded definitions / 152 distinct sūtra IDs across
PG4–PG12. The declared unaccented, unprefixed classical kartari profiles
cover 26/38 class-5, 10/10 class-8 and 70/71 class-9 entries; these counts
do not establish all contexts of those classes or an overall grammar
percentage. [Remaining work](research/panini_grammar/nasal_present/REMAINING.md)
includes other present classes, dhinv/kṛnv's coupled changes, prefix and
register contexts, and wider grammar. PG1–PG11 remain frozen. Full native
operator lifting, typed zero-specialization compatibility and quantum-calculator
integration remain open; hosted CI and main merging remain deferred.

## PG13: causative presents with owned root constituents

[PG13](research/panini_grammar/causative_present/README.md) develops twelve
identified causative roots / twelve P paradigms / 108 present cells. It
extends the useful T105 three-cell examples to all nine cells per root,
with explicit lexical identity and separate causative and pada licenses.
Root vṛddhi, r accompaniment, gam's intermediate gām and mit shortening,
and root-final puk/yuk remain in the history. The augment's k marker and
pronunciation-only u are distinguished. The full root constituent plus
ṇic becomes the derived dhātu; later śap acts on ṇic's own i → e → ay.

The [construction contract](research/panini_grammar/causative_present/CONTRACT.md)
states the finite stage domains and ownership argument.
[Source review](research/panini_grammar/causative_present/SOURCE_REVIEW.md)
binds fifteen records and four source decisions without new scope overlays.
[Concurrent review](research/panini_grammar/causative_present/CONCURRENT_REVIEW.json)
statically confirms the T89 fixes, records useful T105/T107 work and
separates two source-explanation corrections from output claims. Companion
code and certificates are not imported or replayed.

[Validation](research/panini_grammar/causative_present/VALIDATION.md) records
11 literal-word cases, 31 refusals and 74 controls. All 108 comparison cells
and 36 separately labeled T105 expected examples agree. Kṛṣ 1060006's
exact-word witness remains open; previous evidence gaps remain unchanged.
[Current coverage](research/panini_grammar/causative_present/COVERAGE_STATUS.json)
keeps 228 simple-present roots / 2,610 cells separate from the 108 causative
cells: 2,718 cells when formation is part of their identity. PG4–PG13 contain
223 bounded definitions / 161 distinct sūtra IDs. No overall grammar
percentage is assigned.
[Remaining work](research/panini_grammar/causative_present/REMAINING.md)
includes further causative exceptions, semantic pada contexts, wider
formations and the full native operator lift with typed zero-specialization
compatibility. Quantum-calculator integration remains open. PG1–PG12 are
frozen; hosted CI and main merging remain deferred.

## PG14: mathematical grammar, meaning-to-form and native history operators

[PG14](research/panini_grammar/grammar_construction/README.md) develops the
written grammar before another implementation/test batch. Its
[state and composition construction](research/panini_grammar/grammar_construction/STATE_AND_COMPOSITION.md)
retains source evidence, semantic predicates, owned constituents, optional
branches, rule-relative visibility and open obligations. Local soundness
composes; completeness requires the stated factorization and local-coverage
hypotheses.

[Voice and pada](research/panini_grammar/grammar_construction/VOICE_AND_PADA.md)
derive causee kāraka, causative permission and pada on twelve identified
root/sense frames, with principal-result semantics, 1.3.77 optionality and
1.3.86–89 exceptions. Karma-passive agreement follows the expressed karman;
bhāva uses default 3sg even with plural agents. The
[written derivations](research/panini_grammar/grammar_construction/DERIVATIONS.md)
include complete thematic A ending chains, causative A and kriyate/hriyate,
with marker ownership and Tripādī history preserved.

The [native operator lift](research/panini_grammar/grammar_construction/NATIVE_OPERATOR_LIFT.md)
constructs a free noncommutative cut-path coefficient algebra over the native
cut field, history cuts, a declared algebra-valued pairing and lawful typed
rule operators. Its augmentation proves exact zero-recovery of the same
labelled classical derivations; the complementary-channel example shows
why observation compression is a different operation. This establishes an
admitted algebraic instance, not all native carriers, all Sanskrit rule
interpretations or an adapter already implemented by the older Python code.

[Sources](research/panini_grammar/grammar_construction/SOURCE_REVIEW.md) and
[pins](research/panini_grammar/grammar_construction/SOURCE_PINS.json) identify
the adopted readings. [Claude review](research/panini_grammar/grammar_construction/CLAUDE_REVIEW.md)
reads T110/T112–117 and qualifies bhāva plural licensing and T114's
similarity-sense description. The
[work record](research/panini_grammar/grammar_construction/WORK_RECORD.md)
records written proofs and source reading; Python tests, including the
navigation suite, are deferred under the owner's current instruction.
PG1–PG13's implemented coverage, theorem packets and certificates remain
unchanged. [Remaining work](research/panini_grammar/grammar_construction/REMAINING.md)
keeps wider grammar and runtime/native integration open. CI and main merge
remain deferred.

## PG15: causative passive, expressed karman and retained ṇic

[PG15](research/panini_grammar/causative_passive/README.md) continues the
written construction on twelve identified causative root/sense frames.
[Karman selection](research/panini_grammar/causative_passive/DOMAIN_AND_KARMAN.md)
constructs the dependent causee-role/expressed-karman branches, residual
cases and controller agreement. Cognition/ingestion can express either
karman; the adopted hṛ/kṛ policy gives (K,b) or (Agt,c), not every Cartesian
combination. A separate basic double-karman comparison prevents reusing
the causal policy on an unqualified root spelling.

[Owned ṇic-lopa](research/panini_grammar/causative_passive/NIC_LOPA.md)
derives the aniṭ ārdhadhātuka guard of 6.4.51, retains root/augment effects,
and distinguishes nī's root-substitute y from pā's yuk y. The
[written derivations](research/panini_grammar/causative_passive/DERIVATIONS.md)
expand twelve aṅgas into nine A slots and connect selected karmans to
different agreement/case outcomes. Displayed words select the non-doubling
alternatives of 8.4.46–47; they are not a complete phonetic-variant inventory
or 108 new executed cells.

The [native extension](research/panini_grammar/causative_passive/NATIVE_EXTENSION.md)
preserves PG14's old sector, pairing and exact typed zero-reading. It
proves a causal-cut no-descent theorem for the common gamyate word observer
and constructs a one-extra-channel repair with explicit inverse and
transported pairing. This finite repair is not a universal minimum-state
theorem for the whole grammar.

[Source review](research/panini_grammar/causative_passive/SOURCE_REVIEW.md)
attributes the double-karman policy to the commentary procedure stored
under 7.1.69, preserves textual variants and distinguishes literal words
from derived forms. A [targeted T118 review](research/panini_grammar/causative_passive/CLAUDE_T118_REVIEW.md)
reads the later bee6b02 snapshot, accepts its asādṛśya correction and records
source/typing repairs; [Claude's handoff](research/panini_grammar/causative_passive/PARALLEL_WORK.md)
proposes independent samāsa continuation after that progress.
The [work record and frontier](research/panini_grammar/causative_passive/WORK_RECORD.md)
keep Python/navigation tests deferred under the owner's mathematics-first
instruction; PG1–PG14 frozen evidence, prior implemented coverage, the shared engine
and certificates are unchanged. Wider grammar, runtime integration, hosted
CI and main merging remain open or deferred.

## PG16: future meaning, owned sya/tās and complete bounded paradigms

[PG16](research/panini_grammar/future_construction/README.md) constructs
lṛṭ/luṭ on eight exact root/sense entries, twelve root/pada profiles and
216 written person-number positions. The [semantic domain](research/panini_grammar/future_construction/SEMANTIC_DOMAIN.md)
distinguishes future-general from verb-encoded anadyatana, using an
explicit temporal adapter and Nyāsa's word/sentence-meaning distinction.
Principal-result evidence resolves U-root pada; missing evidence is not
free optionality. [Affix construction](research/panini_grammar/future_construction/AFFIX_AND_IT.md)
separates ārdhadhātuka sya/tās from sārvadhātuka tiṅ, and proves the
gam versus kṛ/hṛ iṭ guards and the lawful late-junction factorization.

The [ending construction](research/panini_grammar/future_construction/ENDING_CONSTRUCTION.md)
completes all nine slots, including luṭ A's -āsāthe/-ādhve/-āsvahe,
and retains the query-relative identity of ḍā/rau/ras. [Derivations](research/panini_grammar/future_construction/DERIVATIONS.md)
display every position and give the bounded composition theorem. The
[source review](research/panini_grammar/future_construction/SOURCE_REVIEW.md)
preserves the rival tāsi-i analyses, attributes the selected 8.2.25
procedure and records misattached publisher entries; earlier future
packets are acknowledged and qualified additively.

The [native extension](research/panini_grammar/future_construction/NATIVE_EXTENSION.md)
retains the old corner, pairing, complementary cuts and exact typed
zero-recovery. On a fixed 24-history luṭ sector it proves twelve P/A
word-collision fibres, an explicit repair with transported pairing, and
a twelve-coordinate lower bound for linear repairs with injective zero
reading. This is not a universal memory bound for Sanskrit.

[Work and frontier](research/panini_grammar/future_construction/WORK_RECORD.md)
keep source/written results separate from implementation: no new executed
cells, unchanged historical coverage, and tests/certificate replay deferred
under the owner's instruction. Wider futures, other verbal formations,
runtime integration and main merge remain open or deferred. [Claude's
handoff](research/panini_grammar/future_construction/CLAUDE_HANDOFF.md) assigns
independent samāsa/nominal work. The shared operator engine and frozen
predecessor evidence remain unchanged.

## PG17: dependent future branches and retained causative ṇic

[PG17](research/panini_grammar/future_extension/README.md) adds nine exact
basic-root entries and continues twelve inherited causative frames into
lṛṭ/luṭ. The [joint branch relation](research/panini_grammar/future_extension/DOMAIN_AND_BRANCHES.md)
derives affix- and pada-dependent iṭ options from 7.2.44/48/57/59/60/70.
It distinguishes svṛ's sya override, iṣ versus nṛt's opposite affix
conditions, and the vṛt-group P prohibition. Kṛp has three joint
pada/iṭ branches, not the four elements of an independent product.

[Root derivations](research/panini_grammar/future_extension/ROOT_DERIVATIONS.md)
retain lexical identities through guṇa, char and r→l and state the
selected single-t syand luṭ reading without backward Tripādī feedback.
[Causative continuation](research/panini_grammar/future_extension/CAUSATIVE_FUTURES.md)
preserves PG14's role/pada evidence across 21 causal pada profiles.
Outer iṭ blocks 6.4.51's ṇic-lopa; guṇa and ayādi change ṇic-i
while protecting the distinct augment i. The [paradigm register](research/panini_grammar/future_extension/PARADIGMS.md)
specifies all 79 branch rows with four proved nine-slot constructors:
612 morphological positions and 711 factorized branch readings. These
are written constructions, not executed cells or distinct-word counts.

The [native construction](research/panini_grammar/future_extension/NATIVE_BRANCH_CONSTRUCTION.md)
preserves the old corner, pairing, guard/complement cuts and exact typed
zero-recovery. Its dependent branch operator has exactly the three
licensed kṛp alternatives. A separate observation theorem proves that
the entire P future paradigm cannot distinguish vṛt from vṛdh, while
a forward A query separates this pair. Zero-specialization itself
retains both lexical histories.

[Source review](research/panini_grammar/future_extension/SOURCE_REVIEW.md)
and [pins](research/panini_grammar/future_extension/SOURCE_PINS.json)
retain the exact edition, commentary contexts and inherited qualifications.
[Work and frontier](research/panini_grammar/future_extension/WORK_RECORD.md)
record no new grammar tests or certificate replay under the owner's
mathematics-first instruction. Bhāva/karma futures, wider root inventories,
other verbal formations and runtime integration remain open. The
[companion review](research/panini_grammar/future_extension/COMPANION_REVIEW.md)
records the newly arrived T119/T120, accepts specific T118 repairs and
identifies source/guard corrections for Claude's samāsa/kṛt/nominal lane.

## PG18: bhāva/karma futures, ciṇvat and origin-sensitive queries

[PG18](research/panini_grammar/future_voice/README.md) constructs future
expression of bhāva and karman for sixteen basic entries and thirteen
causal frames. Its [semantic domain](research/panini_grammar/future_voice/SEMANTIC_DOMAIN.md)
separates default Bhāva 3sg from expressed-karman agreement and retains
PG15's dependent causal role selection. It adds the exact class-4 śam
frame without conflating its basic and derived transitivity.

[Ciṇvat and visibility](research/panini_grammar/future_voice/CINVAT_AND_VISIBILITY.md)
derives 6.4.62's coupled choice and iṭ, its restricted aṅga transport
and its priority over an extra ordinary augment. Ordinary iṭ preserves
causative ṇic; the adopted ciṇvat-iṭ view admits 6.4.51 lopa while
retaining the augment itself. Grah's ordinary long ī and ciṇvat short
i provide a second origin-sensitive query. [Basic derivations](research/panini_grammar/future_voice/BASIC_DERIVATIONS.md)
include han/grah/dṛś, the gam A restriction and a local proof of han
luṭ nasal retention under the selected tāsi/6.4.22 policy.

[Causal derivations](research/panini_grammar/future_voice/CAUSATIVE_DERIVATIONS.md)
preserve puk/yuk and construct retained/lopped ṇic interfaces, including
the conditional mit length branch. The [register](research/panini_grammar/future_voice/PARADIGMS.md)
specifies 98 complete nine-slot rows and thirteen restricted single-slot
rows: 458 positions and 895 factorized written morphological readings.
These are not executed cells, independent quotations or distinct words.

The [native construction](research/panini_grammar/future_voice/NATIVE_QUERY_CONSTRUCTION.md)
extends the declared carrier, pairing, old corner and complementary cuts
with exact typed zero-recovery. Its restricted transport theorem retains
actual future-affix/ending types. Its query obstruction proves that the
same raw owned-letter display cannot decide ṇic-lopa after augment
source and branch history have been forgotten; zero-specialization
itself preserves those histories.

[Source review](research/panini_grammar/future_voice/SOURCE_REVIEW.md)
and [pins](research/panini_grammar/future_voice/SOURCE_PINS.json) preserve
commentary choices and written-versus-quoted distinctions. The
[companion update](research/panini_grammar/future_voice/COMPANION_UPDATE.md)
reads the newly arrived T121–T124, recognizes T122's T119/T120 repairs
and identifies the remaining nominal work and aś marker correction.
[Work and frontier](research/panini_grammar/future_voice/WORK_RECORD.md)
keep tests/certificate replay deferred under the owner's instruction;
imperative/optative construction is next, with broader future domains
and runtime integration still open.

## PG19: loṭ/vidhiliṅ, addressed endings and modal information

[PG19](research/panini_grammar/imperative_optative/README.md) constructs a
finite classical imperative/optative domain. Its [meaning relation](research/panini_grammar/imperative_optative/DOMAIN_AND_MODALITY.md)
keeps the six shared senses of 3.3.161–162 distinct and adds the bounded
praiṣa/time/sma profiles of 3.3.163–166. It preserves the expressed
controller and causal role/pada relation; the intended addressee does not
automatically become second-person tiṅ.

The [ending construction](research/panini_grammar/imperative_optative/ENDING_CONSTRUCTION.md)
derives restricted laṅ transport, loṭ hi/ni/āṭ and pitness, and vidhiliṅ
yāsuṭ/sīyuṭ/suṭ with the correct ending owner of ṅit. Its four thematic
constructors feed [basic stems](research/panini_grammar/imperative_optative/THEMATIC_DERIVATIONS.md),
[kṛ/krī's complete athematic paths](research/panini_grammar/imperative_optative/ATHEMATIC_DERIVATIONS.md)
and [causal/voice continuations](research/panini_grammar/imperative_optative/VOICE_AND_CAUSATIVES.md).
Śam's basic, causal active and causal passive optatives have distinct
owned formations. The [register](research/panini_grammar/imperative_optative/PARADIGMS.md)
specifies 108 nine-slot paradigms plus four Bhāva entries: **976 written
morphological positions**, under the declared sound/domain policies.
These are not executed cases, distinct strings or independent quotations.

The [native construction](research/panini_grammar/imperative_optative/NATIVE_MODAL_CONSTRUCTION.md)
retains the full carrier, old corner, pairing and complementary cuts with
exact typed zero-recovery. One two-frame obstruction shows that even both
complete mood paradigms cannot recover obligation versus discretionary
permission after their intended-sense record is forgotten. A second
obstruction shows why marker queries require their addressed affix boundary.
Neither obstruction is caused by zero-specialization, which retains both.

[Source review](research/panini_grammar/imperative_optative/SOURCE_REVIEW.md)
and [pins](research/panini_grammar/imperative_optative/SOURCE_PINS.json)
retain literal-versus-derived evidence, the resolved-augment schedule and
devoiced-pause choice. [Claude's T125–T130 review](research/panini_grammar/imperative_optative/COMPANION_UPDATE.md)
records both tables' finite boundaries, the 90-position T96/T99 modal
overlap, the 3.4.92 pit-state gap and outstanding T124/T125 source repairs.
[Work and frontier](research/panini_grammar/imperative_optative/WORK_RECORD.md)
keep tests deferred; āśīrliṅ/blessing-loṭ, broader roots/modal contexts
and runtime integration remain open. The canonical operator engine is unchanged.

## PG20: āśīrliṅ, blessing loṭ and dependent morphological choices

[PG20](research/panini_grammar/benediction/README.md) constructs the
[āśīs input](research/panini_grammar/benediction/DOMAIN.md) and its two
different affix regimes. The [owned ending construction](research/panini_grammar/benediction/ENDING_CONSTRUCTION.md)
derives the P kit override, conditional A kit, the source argument for
no iṭ before yāsuṭ, distinct canceled/surviving s owners and the late
dh/ḍh alternatives. [Seventeen basic roots](research/panini_grammar/benediction/BASIC_DERIVATIONS.md)
include the source-attested vac/jāgṛ marker contrasts.

[Causatives and voice](research/panini_grammar/benediction/CAUSATIVE_AND_VOICE.md)
continue all thirteen owned causal frames through P ṇic-lopa, active A
retention and passive ciṇvat/mit choices, plus six basic voice inputs.
[Blessing loṭ](research/panini_grammar/benediction/BLESSING_LOT.md) reuses
PG19's ordinary morphology and derives forty-two added tātaṅ readings,
including kurutāt/krīṇītāt with their changed stem premises. The
[complete register](research/panini_grammar/benediction/PARADIGMS.md) has
**1,057 written morphological positions and 1,292 word-branch readings**.
These are not executions, distinct strings or a novelty/completion percentage:
488 ordinary loṭ words reuse PG19, and T115/T117 overlap 26 āśīrliṅ positions.

The [native proof](research/panini_grammar/benediction/NATIVE_BENEDICTION.md)
retains the full carrier, pairing, complementary cuts and typed zero-law.
It constructs dependent branch fibres and proves three local boundaries:
kit-or-ṅit loses an active distinction, an ending-only adapter cannot
repair a previously chosen strong stem, and the word kurutāt alone
does not recover its original person/controller. Histories retain all
three distinctions under zero-specialization.

[Sources](research/panini_grammar/benediction/SOURCE_REVIEW.md) and
[pins](research/panini_grammar/benediction/SOURCE_PINS.json) retain the
selected two-s schedule, local augment visibility and literal-versus-derived
status. [Claude through T135](research/panini_grammar/benediction/COMPANION_UPDATE.md)
recognizes the new laṭ breadth and records the remaining marker/source
integration gaps. [Work and frontier](research/panini_grammar/benediction/WORK_RECORD.md)
keep grammar/navigation/engine tests and certificate replay deferred under
the owner's instruction. Wider athematic modal and contextual liṅ domains
remain open; the canonical operator engine is unchanged.

## PG21: nasal-class modals, source-policy branches and stem transport

[PG21](research/panini_grammar/nasal_modals/README.md) extends the exact
PG6/PG12 root contracts to complete loṭ, vidhiliṅ and blessing-loṭ
continuations. Its [domain](research/panini_grammar/nasal_modals/DOMAIN.md)
has seventeen roots and twenty-seven qualified pada profiles. The five
3.1.82 roots retain both śnu/śnā branches; four tanādi roots retain
the separately labelled commentary guṇa policies, rather than treating
that disagreement as an unquoted universal optional rule.

[Owned stems](research/panini_grammar/nasal_modals/STEM_CONSTRUCTION.md)
and [ending constructors](research/panini_grammar/nasal_modals/MODAL_ENDINGS.md)
derive root n-lopa, actual-cluster hi-luk, śnu uv/v versus u yaṇ,
śnā weakness and the first-person loṭ āṭ/pit consequences. They prove
why laṭ's direct m/v u-lopa supplies no extra modal branch here.
[Derivations](research/panini_grammar/nasal_modals/DERIVATIONS.md) cover
the complete rule-sensitive cases; the [register](research/panini_grammar/nasal_modals/PARADIGMS.md)
has **729 written positions / 1,159 source-family readings**, including
52 tātaṅ branch additions. Fixing one of the two guṇa policies for
each affected root gives 935 readings. Ordinary blessing-loṭ words
overlap the ordinary loṭ words; these are not distinct-string or novelty totals.

The [native construction](research/panini_grammar/nasal_modals/NATIVE_TRANSPORT.md)
retains the full carrier, pairing, complementary cuts and typed zero-law.
It proves actual-cluster and affix-identity observation boundaries, then
an exact rank-two comparison of three laṭ branches with two loṭ branches.
A history-retaining target repairs the lost donor distinction; zero itself
does not erase it. No universal mood-transport bijection is asserted.

[Sources](research/panini_grammar/nasal_modals/SOURCE_REVIEW.md) and
[pins](research/panini_grammar/nasal_modals/SOURCE_PINS.json) retain the
selected schedules, source-policy family and inherited word-witness gaps.
[Claude's handoff](research/panini_grammar/nasal_modals/COMPANION_UPDATE.md)
recognizes T133–T135 and identifies the existing primary answer to its
tanādi guṇa question. [Work/frontier](research/panini_grammar/nasal_modals/WORK_RECORD.md)
records deferred grammar/navigation/engine tests and certificate replay;
adādi/juhotyādi/rudhādi modal and remaining contextual liṅ domains are next.
The canonical operator engine remains unchanged.

## PG22: athematic modal continuations and dependent consonant fibres

[PG22](research/panini_grammar/athematic_modals/README.md) extends
PG7–PG9's exact adādi/juhotyādi/rudhādi contracts to loṭ, vidhiliṅ
and blessing loṭ. Its [domain](research/panini_grammar/athematic_modals/DOMAIN.md)
contains twelve roots / eighteen qualified pada profiles, with actor,
meaning, source policy and controller retained. [Stem and ending contracts](research/panini_grammar/athematic_modals/STEMS_AND_ENDINGS.md)
use actual kit/ṅit/pit prescriptions, owned śap-luk/ślu/śnam and
the new augment contexts rather than copying laṭ slot flags.

[Hi and visibility](research/panini_grammar/athematic_modals/HI_AND_VISIBILITY.md)
derive dhi, dehi/dhehi/edhi, whole-abhyāsa lopa and the original-s
query that remains valid after etva. [Consonant readout](research/panini_grammar/athematic_modals/CONSONANT_READOUT.md)
retains dhā's bhaṣ exception, root-num/affix-n ownership, ordered nasal
views and every eligible optional collapse. The [complete register](research/panini_grammar/athematic_modals/PARADIGMS.md)
has **486 positions / 558 written branch readings**: 183 ordinary
loṭ, 162 vidhiliṅ and 213 blessing-loṭ readings. Twenty-four tāt
positions receive thirty added readings because three roots acquire
two-word sound fibres. Ordinary blessing and ordinary loṭ words overlap.

The [native construction](research/panini_grammar/athematic_modals/NATIVE_ATHEMATIC.md)
starts with the full carrier, pairing and complementary cuts, establishes
typed zero-recovery, proves the current-e observation obstruction and
constructs the two-choice/three-output rudh incidence operator. Copying
an ordinary ending's collapse flag loses a licensed new tāt word;
zero-specialization itself retains the needed histories.

[Source review](research/panini_grammar/athematic_modals/SOURCE_REVIEW.md)
and [pins](research/panini_grammar/athematic_modals/SOURCE_PINS.json)
preserve local visibility assumptions and literal edition anomalies.
[Claude through T138](research/panini_grammar/athematic_modals/COMPANION_UPDATE.md)
records the new adādi present work, reported modal gaps and concrete marker-guard
handoff. [Work/frontier](research/panini_grammar/athematic_modals/WORK_RECORD.md)
keeps grammar/navigation/operator tests and certificate replay deferred.
Remaining adādi modal exceptions, contextual liṅ meanings, broader
voice/causative transport and general visibility resolution stay open.
The canonical operator engine and frozen evidence remain unchanged.

**2,070 positions, 3,091 readings/paths and 230 complete paradigms**,
cumulative **21,641**; static Python 3.12 checks only. The proposed
ud/ā-yam frontier is uncounted and grammar tests/replay remain deferred.

## PG327 · Ā + han aorist and selected 90-request closure

[Owned derivation, source-policy boundary and complete register](research/panini_grammar/aa_han_own_limb_aorist_and_selected_grid/README.md). Eight new finite positions and sixteen readings complete the selected 90/90 own-limb grid. The printed seṭ 2pl is bound to the declared Bālamanoramā aṅga account; Nyāsa reconciliation and three digital-source strings remain open. Cumulative **51,245**. The full carrier retains sic/tiṅ/iṭ ownership, both optional root histories and source-policy guards through typed zero-specialization. Written closure does not imply executable or whole-language completion.

## PG326 · Ā + han blessing: compulsory vadha and owned s occurrences

[Grammar and native adapter](research/panini_grammar/aa_han_own_limb_benedictive_completion/README.md). Eight new finite positions/readings complete the selected blessing panel, with one new literal control and seven written compositions. The a-final vadha substitute and sīyuṭ/suṭ owners preserve the distinction from luṅ-only optionality. Cumulative **51,237**, bounded own-limb target **82/90**. Typed zero-recovery uses the pinned full operator/history construction; executable grammar remains deferred.

## PG325 · Ā + han perfect and the printed first plural

[Grammar and source collation](research/panini_grammar/aa_han_own_limb_perfect_and_printed_table/README.md). Eight new written finite positions/readings complete the selected perfect panel. The scan controls all nine word shapes; its first plural is ājaghnimahe and the own-limb semantic licence is inherited separately. Cumulative **51,229**, bounded own-limb target **74/90**. The full native history carrier and typed zero-recovery are reused without a runtime or external-verification upgrade.

## PG324 · Ā + han: the remaining present-system persons

[Grammar and full-carrier adapter](research/panini_grammar/aa_han_own_limb_present_system_completion/README.md). Eighteen new written finite positions/readings complete the three selected imperfect/imperative/optative panels. Six new word-shape controls are literal; twelve cells are compositions. The own-limb target reaches 66/90 and the cumulative finite total is **51,221**. The unassigned digital āhanva string remains open for printed collation. The pinned PG14 full operator/history carrier supplies typed zero-recovery, pairing and complementary cuts for the declared new guarded events; no executable-grammar claim is made.

Catalogue coverage, written derivations and executable verification remain separate; grammar execution and certificate replay are deferred.

ID registers remain **3,983**; grammar execution and replay are deferred.

## PG65: Jiri continuations and owned sic aorists

[PG65](research/panini_grammar/jiri_ardhadhatuka/README.md) adds **378 written
positions and 416 readings** across 42 complete paradigms, taking the total
to **12,452**. Jiri's reported source policy supports the selected perfect,
future, causal, desiderative and passive continuations. Basic and san sic
aorists for ciri/jiri distinguish original root-vowel strengthening, san
a-lopa history, affix iṭ and ending īṭ. The [source decision](research/panini_grammar/jiri_ardhadhatuka/SOURCE_DECISIONS.md)
keeps causal caṅ's multi-vowel scope explicit and uncounted. Both inherited
ID registers retain **3,983**. The [native construction](research/panini_grammar/jiri_ardhadhatuka/DOMAIN_AND_NATIVE.md)
proves typed zero/pairing compatibility on admitted and held histories.
Changed-document checks are separate from deferred grammar execution.

## PG64: Ciri continuations and the reported wider jiri scope

[PG64](research/panini_grammar/ciri_continuations/README.md) adds **360 finite

and passive continuations preserve the different fates of root i, ṇic-i and
san-final a. Four jiri paradigms use the scope attributed to some teachers in
the commentary on *Dhātukāvya* 2.71. The [source collation](research/panini_grammar/ciri_continuations/SOURCE_DECISIONS.md)

is **12,074 positions**, with **3,983 IDs** in each inherited rule register.

preserves source, role and affix identity through zero specialization.

requests remain held. The [native continuation](research/panini_grammar/ciri_jiri_rksi_sources/DOMAIN_AND_NATIVE.md)
retains source/role cuts, auxiliary ownership, ordered histories and
typed zero/pairing compatibility. Python 3.12 changed-document checks

suites. Frozen packets and the canonical runtime are unchanged.

## PG58: ख्शा/क्शा visibility and the effective PG27 vṛj correction

explicit 3.1.52 visibility permission.

[Effective PG27 correction](research/panini_grammar/khsha_visibility/DERIVATIONS.md)
changes non-num class-2 G = vraj to **varj**: varjai, varjāvahai,

or changing its counts. [Native continuation](research/panini_grammar/khsha_visibility/DOMAIN_AND_NATIVE.md)

compatibility. [T163 review](research/panini_grammar/khsha_visibility/COMPANION_REVIEW.md)

retains both **3,983-ID sets / 11,552 positions**. Python 3.12 document
checks only; grammar tests, generators, replay and navigation/operator
suites remain deferred. The next written question is the tanādi root-owned
उ versus vikaraṇa account for ṛṇu/tṛṇu/ghṛṇu.

शानच् target instead retains चक्ष्. The source's आख्येयम् example
shows why intended-domain substitution precedes root-dependent suffix choice.
The substitute's ञ् qualifies pada admission independently of the old root.

units** across addressed instructions, designation/deletion consumers,

remain **3,983/3,983**; finite positions remain **11,552**. New ID,
position and counted-paradigm-reading credit is zero.

The [interface](research/panini_grammar/interaction_histories/INTERFACE.md) preserves revision-bound
admission, token and sound-version addresses, source-clause/policy origins,
consumer views and Open obligations. [Instruction](research/panini_grammar/interaction_histories/INSTRUCTION_HISTORIES.md),
[designation](research/panini_grammar/interaction_histories/DESIGNATION_HISTORIES.md),
[competition](research/panini_grammar/interaction_histories/COMPETITION_HISTORIES.md) and
[realization histories](research/panini_grammar/interaction_histories/REALIZATION_HISTORIES.md)
include coupled मनु branches, inner-affix replacement and the prerequisite
टि deletion for औडुलोम्या. The [register](research/panini_grammar/interaction_histories/HISTORY_REGISTER.json)
records each local scope; these are not executed cases or full paradigms.

[Source review](research/panini_grammar/interaction_histories/SOURCE_REVIEW.md),

retains the full carrier and typed zero-reading under inherited assumptions.

PG46's 135 rows, 902 literals and 41 pins; [coverage](research/panini_grammar/interaction_histories/COVERAGE.json)
preserves the exact inherited ID sets. [Handoff/work](research/panini_grammar/interaction_histories/HANDOFF_AND_WORK.md)
and [frontier](research/panini_grammar/interaction_histories/FRONTIER.md) target the nested
4.1.78→4.1.120→6.4.151 provenance/य-lopa history next. The companion remains
